ParlaMint-SI_2015-07-14-SDZ7-Redna-10.ana.u1	Dear fellow Members and fellow Members, ladies and gentlemen! I'm about to resume the 10th session of the National Assembly. I am informed that today's meeting cannot be attended by the following Member and Members: Mag. Tanja Zink, Mr Kamal Izidor Shaker, Mr Marijan Pojbič and Mrs Vojka Shergan by 16:00. I say hello to everyone who's here! We're moving to four. On a daily basis, this is a proposal for a law on amendments to the law on environmental protection by emergency procedure. The draft law was tabled by the Government for consideration by the National Assembly. In order to further explain the proposal, I give the floor to the representative of the Government, the Minister of the Environment and the premises of Mrs Irena Majcen.
ParlaMint-SI_2015-07-14-SDZ7-Redna-10.ana.u2	Dear President of the National Assembly, honourable Members, honourable Members, all present! The proposed law amending the Environmental Protection Act deletes the provisions of Article 172, paragraph 3, and Article 172, paragraph 4, of the Environmental Protection Act. The main purpose of deleting these provisions is to fulfil the commitment of the Republic of Slovenia to the requirements of the European Commission, in order to implement the judgment of the Court of Justice of the European Union in case C-49/10 of 2010. By this judgment, the Court found that the Republic of Slovenia was in breach of Directive 2008/1/EC because, by 30 October 2007, it had not taken the necessary measures to operate the existing installations on that day with appropriate environmental permits. Since the Republic of Slovenia has failed to fulfil its obligations under that judgment of the Court of Justice of the European Union, as a number of devices without a legal environmental permit still operated, the European Commission is 25. Ten. 2012 sent an official reminder to the Republic of Slovenia in which it invited it to implement the judgment of the Court of Justice of the European Union. In doing so, the Commission also drew attention to the provision of Article 172, paragraph 3, of the Environmental Protection Act, since it was precisely because of this provision of the existing installation that they could operate without an environmental permit. The Republic of Slovenia replied extensively to the European Commission's claim by letter. It is also the European Commission in 2013, 27. 9. has sent an official notice for incorrect transposition of certain provisions of Directive 2008/1/EC, which also lists a violation of the provisions of Article 5(1) of the Directive, also in conjunction with the provisions of Article 172, third paragraph, of the Environmental Protection Act. The Republic of Slovenia has also responded extensively to this reminder, namely 26. 11. 2013 The European Commission insists on its claims under those official reminders and requires the provisions of Article 172, paragraph 3, to be deleted. By adopting the proposed amendment of the law, the Republic of Slovenia may avoid effectively bringing an action before a court and in the event of a finding of violation of the penalty for default. The penalty at stake is a round one, a million 600 000 euros and 9,000 euros a day until the execution of the judgment. In the second article of the proposed amendments, we are also correcting the error in the previous provisions of the Law on Amendments and Supplements to the Environmental Protection Act, whereby operators of installations that may cause pollution of a larger scale should submit a baseline report on soil and groundwater conditions at any time on any proposed change from their installation or any change to the environmental permit that would take place after official duty. Consequently, the provisions of Article 84(2) of the Law on Amendments and Supplements to the Environmental Protection Act shall be amended in such a way as to ensure that this obligation occurs only with any intended major modification of the device. Thank you.
ParlaMint-SI_2015-07-14-SDZ7-Redna-10.ana.u3	The proposal for a law was considered by the Committee on Infrastructure, Environment and Space as the working body of the mother. For the presentation of the report of the Committee, I give the floor to President Igor Zorčič.
ParlaMint-SI_2015-07-14-SDZ7-Redna-10.ana.u4	Mr President, thank you for the floor. The Committee on Infrastructure, Environment and Space is at its 18th emergency session. Seven. 2015 as a parent working body has considered the proposal for a law amending the Environmental Protection Act submitted by the Government to the National Assembly for consideration following an emergency procedure. The College of the President of the National Assembly shall be held at its 32nd meeting, on 2. Seven. 2015 decided that the proposal for a law should be considered on an urgent basis. Representatives of the Ministry of Environment and Space, the Commission of the State Council for Local Self-Government and Regional Development and the Legislative and Legal Service participated in the meeting of the Committee. On behalf of the proposer, the Minister for Environment and Space, Irena Majcen, stressed that the main purpose of the bill is to comply with the European Commission's 2010 European Court of Justice's requirements. By judgment, the Court found that the Republic of Slovenia was in breach of Council Directive 96/61/EU of 24. 9. 1996 on integrated pollution prevention and control, since up to 31. Ten. 2007 did not take the necessary measures to ensure that existing installations capable of causing more pollution operate on that day with appropriate environmental permits. Even after that judgment, the Republic of Slovenia failed to fulfil its obligations, as a number of devices were still operating without a legal environmental permit and the European Commission was therefore 25. Ten. 2012 sent a letter of formal notice to the Republic of Slovenia urging it to execute its judgment, since it is precisely because of this provision of the existing device that it is possible to operate without an environmental permit. The European Commission is 27. 9. 2013 also sent an official reminder to the Republic of Slovenia for incorrect transposition of certain provisions of Directive 2008/1/EC of the European Parliament and of the Council of 15. 1. 2008 on the comprehensive prevention and control of pollution, by which Directive 96/61/EC has been implemented, which also refers to a violation of the provisions of Article 5(1) of the Directive, also in conjunction with the provisions of Article 172, third paragraph, of the Environmental Protection Act. The adoption of the proposed law may prevent the Republic of Slovenia from effectively bringing an action before the Court of Justice of the European Union and in the event of a finding of a violation of the penalty for failure to comply with its obligations. It has already been pointed out below that the draft law also removes the error in the previous provisions of the Law on Amendments and Supplements to the Environmental Protection Act, whereby operators of devices which may cause environmental pollution of a greater extent should submit a baseline report on the state of soil and groundwater on each intended change of their installation or any change in the environmental permit that would take place on an official basis. The representative of the Legal and Legislative Service stressed at the meeting of the Committee that the Legislative and Legal Service had examined the proposal of the law in terms of its compliance with the Constitution of the Republic of Slovenia, the legal system and the legislative technical aspect. It pointed out that there were no comments on the individual provisions of the legislative and legal service proposal, but the opinion pointed out that it was not clear from the explanation of the article whether there were still any proceedings currently under way on that legal basis, since in such a case the termination of that legal basis could have legal consequences for ongoing proceedings. She found that the representative of the applicant gave an unexplained warning in the context of the introductory presentation to the draft law. The President of the Commission of the Council of State for Local Self-Government and Regional Development stressed that the Commission supports the bill and has no comments on it. The discussion expressed the view that the infringement of the draft law on the deletion of the provision of Article 172, paragraph 3, which also permits the operation of those devices which have not obtained an environmental permit if the reasons for the failure to comply with the obligations of the operator on the part of the competent ministry are, in principle, appropriate. In accordance with Rule 128 of the Rules of Procedure of the National Assembly, the Committee voted and adopted all the articles of the bill together. Thank you.

ParlaMint-SI_2015-07-14-SDZ7-Redna-10.ana.u6	Dear President, Minister, colleagues and colleagues! The 1993 Environmental Protection Act was renewed at the time of Slovenia's entry into the EU and has been amended and supplemented six times. In autumn 2013, the European Commission sent an official reminder to the Republic of Slovenia for incorrect transposition of certain provisions of Directive 2008/1/EC of the European Parliament and of the Council on comprehensive pollution prevention and control. Even without warning The European Commission should ensure that the then government prevents over-pollution of air, soil and groundwater, especially in the area of large industrial facilities, and it is therefore unclear why it has not amended and supplemented the law in question in spite of judgements and reminders. Thus, in February this year, the Republic of Slovenia has not yet complied with its obligations to ensure that all installations operate in accordance with that Directive on pollution prevention and control, and the European Commission has decided to reopen proceedings against the Republic of Slovenia before the Court of Justice of the European Union. Article 172 of the Environmental Protection Act lays down an environmental permit for existing installations which may lead to pollution of a larger scale. The deletion of the third and related fourth paragraph of this Article shall constitute the abolition of the legal basis for the application of special benefits in the procedures for obtaining an environmental protection authorisation even where the reasons for failure to fulfil the obligations of the operator are on the part of the Ministry. In the SMC Group, we also support the amendment of the Environmental Protection Act because it prevents financial consequences related to the failure to comply with the requirements of the EU Court of Justice judgment in Case C49 10. This effect would have been borne by the Republic of Slovenia and would have resulted if the amendment of the law had not been adopted. In the long term, however, it seems even more important that existing installations without an environmental permit cease their operation, at the same time, in the event of a major change in the device in accordance with national and European industrial emission regulations, these devices will have to carry out an environmental impact assessment. The very negative impact on the natural and social environment of these devices is what has also encouraged the necessary changes to the law. Thank you.

ParlaMint-SI_2015-07-14-SDZ7-Redna-10.ana.u18	Your Honor! The amendments to the Water Act, which we adopted this March in the tasks of our government, have been dealt with with in all seriousness by our colleagues at the Ministry of Environment and Space. Water is an area where we can all agree that it is not well arranged. The change that we have on the table today governs the most urgent at this time. Given that water is a source of survival, a sustainable source of development, and that inadequate water management poses a threat to people's lives and property damage, the government has instructed us to prepare proposals for measures to reorganise water management. In recent years, unfortunately, a number of flood events have taken place that have resulted in great damage. In order to manage water effectively, it is necessary to establish a water management system in a way that enables effective, integrated and integrated water management in Slovenia, so that we recognize water as a development opportunity and not only as a barrier and a threat to space. The main purpose of the amendment or addition to the current Water Act is thus to provide a legal basis for the implementation of the reorganisation in the field of water management, namely the establishment of a Directorate of the Republic of Slovenia for Water as an Authority in the composition of the Ministry of Environment and Space. The reorganisation in the field of water management will be carried out by combining management tasks in the field of water and transferring certain powers to the Ministry or Directorate. In accordance with the delegation of power, the Ministry or body in the composition of the Ministry will take over the staff of the Institute for Waters of the Republic of Slovenia. To this end, the tasks of the Institute, which are defined by law, should be amended accordingly, while the powers of the Water Directorate will be further determined by amending and supplementing the Regulation on Organs in the composition of the ministries, which is already in preparation. In doing so, I reiterate that none of the staff at the Institute will lose their jobs as a result. No less important is the regulation of the issue of water use or the extension of the transitional period in respect of the payment back for water use for 2014 and 2015. These are the corrective measures recorded in the audit report by the Court of Auditors in 2011. It is an equalization of obligations between those who have already had water rights and paid concessions and those who have not been granted concessions or have not paid for water rights. The issue under consideration is also dealt with by the Ministry as a priority at systemic level by granting water rights. She's working a lot. The draft law also contains the provision of a legal basis for the implementation of exceptional measures in cases where, after a natural disaster, there is still an increased level of risk due to the harmful operation of the water. It is therefore a matter of implementing exceptional measures carried out by operators of the economic public water management service when the intervention under the rules on natural disasters is terminated, but there is still an increased level of threat due to the harmful operation of the water. Furthermore, the proposal contains the implementation of the Government's measures for the budgetary rebalance with regard to the reduction of the costs for the functioning of municipalities in the area of the collection of building rights compensation. The proposal for a law includes the possibility that, under the Water Act, the way in which water and coastal land are made available is initially determined, as well as the burden of these land with real rights. A legal basis is added for the preparation of a methodology to determine the value of water and coastal land and the burden on real rights. On the basis of the Regulation, exemptions will be laid down in respect of the payment of charges for real rights on water and coastal land in the case of the construction of municipal public infrastructure, such as bridges and other water flow crossings. In conclusion, I welcome the amendment to delete the amendment to Article 3 of the Law, which deals with compensation for reduced income from agricultural activities. Thank you.

This Decision shall enter into force on the twentieth day following that of its publication in the Official Journal of the European Union. The reorganisation in the field of water management will be carried out through the merger of management tasks in the field of water and the transfer of certain powers from the Institute of Waters of the Republic of Slovenia to the Ministry or to the new body in the composition of the Ministry, namely the Directorate of the Republic of Slovenia for Water. With regard to this issue, it has been seeking for several years an appropriate arrangement to replace the existing system of payment of refunds under the current regulations on the method of payment and the criteria for calculating the remuneration for the reduction of income from agricultural activities with a view to adapting to the measures of the water protection scheme. The representative of the Legal and Legislative Service pointed out that certain comments from the Legal and Legislative Services were taken into account in the amendments of the Coalition Group and that further clarifications were provided by the applicant in relation to some of the comments. In doing so, she drew attention to Article 3 and stressed that, despite the amendments tabled, not all the confusion was being overcome, as the proposed regime still suggests that compensation should be intended for loss or reduction of income from agricultural activities and, on the other hand, a reduction in the value of land and agricultural holdings should be taken into account when determining the compensation. The criteria for determining the amount of compensation in the law should also be clearly laid down and probably also linked to the type of water protection measure. The President of the Commission of the National Council for Local Self-Governmental and Regional Development, Jernej Verbic, stressed that the Commission supports the proposal for a law. In the debate, some opposition Members drew attention to the opinions transmitted to the Committee regarding the content of the bill by various institutions or the public concerned or to the fact that the content was not adequately aligned with all stakeholders. The most controversial was with regard to Article 3 of the draft law or Article 79 of the existing law and with regard to Article 16 of the bill relating to the transfer of the bulk of employees from the Water Institute to the future water directive. ParlaMint-SI_2015-07-14-SDZ7-Redna-10.ana.u21	The following is a presentation of the views of the groups of Members. The text is by the Slovenian Democratic Party's Group of Members, Mr Daniel Krivec.
ParlaMint-SI_2015-07-14-SDZ7-Redna-10.ana.u22	Thank you for your word, Madam President. Dear Minister, colleagues and colleagues! In the SDS Group, we have proposed that the proposal for a law on amendments and additions to the Water Act be considered on a regular basis and not by a reduced procedure. We are once again faced with the practice that an important system law governing the water sector is proposed for treatment in an unusual way. Perhaps this would be justified if it would happen immediately after the floods, if some pressing problems were also to be addressed at the legislative level. At the moment, there was no need to make changes with these short-term solutions without the need for a broader discussion of all involved, as the debate on the parent committee also showed. Past experience in the adoption of comparable legislation gives rise to our concerns, as the problems posed by poorly conceived solutions later pose a major obstacle to the implementation of law in practice, and many times thankable material for the application of legal protection requirements and constitutional review. The reduced procedure is permissible when it comes to less demanding changes and legislative additions. It is clear from the proposed amendments that there are major changes and new systemic solutions that should not be dealt with in the short term. The content of the explanation of the proposed amendment of the law by the proposer also confirms this. The current state of play in the field of water management is considered unsatisfactory in terms of organisation, which is also confirmed in practice. Therefore, the proposed reorganisation in the field of water management is carried out by combining management tasks in the field of water and transferring certain powers to the Ministry or to the Directorate of the Republic of Slovenia for Water as a body within the Ministry. In accordance with the delegation of power, the Ministry or body in the composition of the Ministry will also take over the staff of the Institute for Water. With the proposed solution to the extension of the transitional period for payment for water use for 2014 and 2015, the payment shall be extended on the grounds that not all concessional acts have yet been granted and the Water Right Payment Regulation granted by water licence has not yet been adopted. In this case, it is only a matter of extending the rights already laid down by the law and, on the other hand, it is necessary to ask why not all the activities and regulations already adopted have been carried out, which are already prescribed by the current law. The Ministry advocates the view that compensation for water and coastal land should be regulated at systemic level. It is reasonable, however, to charge symbolic charges for objects crossing watercourses, service and building rights. The proposed amendments to the law provide a legal basis for implementation, while at the same time preparing a regulation for the establishment of compensation for services and building rights in water and coastal areas. The Regulation should provide for an exception agreed with representatives of local communities to determine the amount of the compensation for the construction of local infrastructure facilities at 5% of the amount of the other compensation. The Water Act regulates the country's tasks in the event of water damage and the recovery of consequences. As regards intervention, it lays down only a few basic provisions on water protection and provides for the adoption of a guidance on concrete intervention measures. Not in the Water Act, but in the Law on Protection against Natural and Other Disasters, the period following the completion of the intervention is not regulated. In the proposed amendment to the law, it shall be governed by a period prior to the implementation of the recovery after the completion of the intervention under the Law on Protection against Natural and Other Accidents, which has so far been unregulated. The Ministry should invite the professional and other public to deliver their opinions and comments in order to draw up such important rules. In the past, in order to prevent corruption, the Commission has repeatedly pointed out the inadequacy of adopting such important legislation by means of a reduced or urgent procedure. Water management has a spatial, infrastructure and environmental dimension. The current organisation of this area is more subordinate to the environmental dimension and infrastructure is extremely inefficient, and it is even more important that we adopt laws in this area with due consideration. Dear colleagues and colleagues! Let me stress that we do not oppose the amendments to the Water Act. On the contrary. New solutions are necessary. We are pleased that the coalition has listened to the substantive debate and warnings on the Committee and has proposed several amendments that we have already proposed on the Committee. In case the amendments are accepted, we will support the law in the SDS. Finally, I must point out that it is confirmed that important laws are being dealt with according to normal procedure in order to avoid the adoption of bad solutions.

ParlaMint-SI_2015-07-14-SDZ7-Redna-10.ana.u24	Thank you for your word, Madam President. Dear Minister with fellow Members, honourable Members and Members of Parliament! In preparing the draft amendment to the Water Act, the proposer pursued two main solutions. The first is the extension of the compensation for the use of water by system, as originally written in the Water Act 2013, with an extension of the period for 2014 and 2015. An extension of the period is necessary because concessionary acts have not yet been granted in full and, in addition to that, a water right payment regulation has not yet been adopted which is granted by a water permit. This solution ensures equal treatment for all those who need water and do not yet have water rights, achieves budgetary sustainability, while making operators significantly less burdened. In particular, the taxpayers of the compensation are users of water for the production of beverages and baths that are in use and have not yet obtained a concession or a water licence and will have to pay compensation for the use of water for 2014 and 2015. However, water users for the production of beverages which have been granted a water permit for the production of beverages will have to pay for the right to water in the case of water licences issued for 2014 and 2015 and the legal or natural person referred to in Article 199.b, fourth paragraph, which was authorised for the production of beverages withdrawn from drinking water facilities and installations issued between 22 December 2013 and 31. This Decision shall enter into force on the The second main solution is the reorganisation of water management on the basis of a decision adopted by the Government on 5 March 2015. The reorganisation in the field of water management will be carried out through the merger of management tasks in the field of water and the transfer of certain powers from the Institute of Waters of the Republic of Slovenia to the Ministry or to the new body in the composition of the Ministry, the Directorate of the Republic of Slovenia for Water. In accordance with the delegation of power, the Ministry or body in the composition of the Ministry will take over certain staff of the Institute of Water. In accordance with the above, the tasks of the institute, which are legally determined, should be amended accordingly, while the powers of the future water directorate will be determined in more detail by amending and supplementing the Authority Regulation in the composition of the ministries. This proposed water management reorganisation is strongly opposed by employees at the Water Institute, who, together with the Education and Education Union, science and culture of Slovenia, point out the problem of the proposed method of transfer of employees from the Water Institute to the Ministry. According to our assessment, the proposed reorganisation does not constitute a distortion of the competence and mission of the Institute of Water, but the professionally strengthened ministry can certainly contribute to a more efficient water management system. Of course, it is clear that employees at the Institute of Waters that will be transferred to the Ministry will retain all rights under the employment contract and other rights and labour obligations. In the Group of the Democratic Party of Pensioners of Slovenia, we also consider that the law is not a suitable place to define the precise systemisation of jobs and organisation of work, but, as is the case with other similar situations, the basic provision on the transfer of work of employees, jobs, equipment and documentation must be introduced into the draft law. The same solutions have already been used, for example, when employees were taken over from outside the State Administration to the Ministry of Labour, Family, Social Affairs and Equal Opportunities, in taking over employees from the Employment Office of the Republic of Slovenia. The appropriateness of the way in which employees are taken over in this particular case was also confirmed by the judgment of the Supreme Court of the Republic of Slovenia. Since we believe that the proposed solutions are appropriate in the Group of the Democratic Party of Pensioners of Slovenia, we will support the proposal for a new law. At the same time, I announce my support for the coalition's amendments to Articles 3, 13, 14 and 17, while we will not support the other amendments proposed in our Group. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-SI_2015-07-14-SDZ7-Redna-10.ana.u34	Dear Mr President, distinguished Minister, co-workers, colleagues and colleagues! The current situation in the field of water management tasks has long been seen as unsatisfactory from the point of view of organisation. This has occurred in the past in particular at times of floods. The Government therefore ordered the Ministry of Environment and Space, in cooperation with the Ministry of Public Administration, to carry out all the necessary activities in order to bring together all management tasks in the field of water and to establish a Directorate of the Republic of Slovenia for Water as an Authority in the composition of the Ministry of Environment and Space and the independent budgetary consumer, which is to begin its operation on 1 January 2016. In addition, the Government ordered the Ministry of Environment and Space to submit to it by May 2015 a law on amendments and additions to the Water Act, which we are dealing with today. The law will thus determine the competences of the RS Institute for Waters to be adequately adapted and transferred to the Directorate. In accordance with the delegation of power, the Directorate will also take over the staff of the Institute. The tasks of the Institute shall be amended accordingly by law and the powers of the Directorate shall be specified in more detail by amending and regulating the Authority Regulation in the composition of the ministries. In addition to the reorganisation, the law also provides for an extension of the previous period for the payment of compensation for water use, since the current regime covers only the period from 2005 to 31. 12. The Law therefore extends the period of payment for 2014 and 2015. With the proposed solution, the payment for the refund, as already regulated under the Water Supplement Act 2013, is extended because not all concessional acts have yet been granted and the Water Rights Payment Regulation has not yet been adopted when granted a water permit. Payment shall be made for the period of actual use of water in 2014 and 2015, on the basis of a concession contract or annex or in the case of water licences based on a decision. The introduction of a refund provides for inflows to the Water Fund for 2014 and 2015 totalling EUR 4 million for water use for drinking and EUR 4.4 million for bathing, heating, etc. The law also provides for equal treatment of water users in 2014 and 2015 during this period in terms of payments for water use. The law also ensures the implementation of the Government's measure to reduce the costs for the functioning of municipalities in the area of the collection of building rights fees. The proposed amendments to the law provide a legal basis for the exemption from the Real Estate Act of the State and the local communities of building rights on water and coastal land and other treatment of these land. The methodology for determining compensation for building rights and other management of water and coastal land in the management of the Ministry will be laid down by a regulation providing that the amount of compensation for construction of local infrastructure facilities and municipal connections to public infrastructure directly linked to water and coastal space will be set at a symbolic rate of 5 % of the amount of other compensation for building rights on those land. The law also provides a legal basis for carrying out all activities at the time of the increased level of risk or after the end of the intervention. With regard to intervention in the event of water damage, the law in force provides for only a few basic provisions on water protection and provides for the adoption of a directive setting out concrete intervention measures, the modalities for their implementation and the functioning of the public service adopted by the Minister responsible for the environment and the Minister for Defence. Everything else is left to the regime under the Law on Protection against Natural and Other Disasters. Intervention under the Law on Protection against Natural and Other Accidents shall be initiated on the basis of an order and concluded by a decision. The problem is that water interventions are not yet completed when intervention under the Law on Protection against Natural and Other Accidents is completed. Not in the Water Act, but in the Law on Protection against Natural and Other Disasters, the period following the completion of the intervention and the prevention of recovery is not regulated. The proposed amendment of the law thus regulates the period following the completion of the intervention and before the recovery. Following a meeting of the committee, the draft law was subsequently coalition and intersectorally coordinated. As a result of the coordination, amendments have been tabled by the coalition parliamentary groups for consideration at the meeting of the National Assembly. For all of this, the SMC Group will support the Law on Water Amendments and Amendments of Coalition Groups on amendments and amendments to the Water Act. Thank you for your word.

As I have already said in my presentation on the position of the SMC Group, the proposal for a law following the last meeting of the committee was still a coalition and inter-resort coordination and the amendment to Article 3 is the result of this coordination. What are the substantive reasons for this amendment? The first is, of course, the one which also stems from the explanation for this amendment, which refers to the comments of the Legislative and Legal Service of the National Assembly concerning the system governing State aid for compensation. The government has decided in its interdepartmental coordination to notify the amended provisions of the law for the sake of legal certainty to the European Commission, and until then we propose that this article be excluded from the consideration of the bill. At the same time, it committed itself to coming forward without delay after harmonising the text and after obtaining the opinion of the European Commission to prepare a new proposal for the amendment of the law and to submit it to the National Assembly for adoption, so that the issue could be addressed in 2016 outside the system of state aid granted. In this regard, I would like to draw attention to the opinion of the two ministries in this inter-departmental coalition coordination. The first is the opinion of the Ministry of Finance, which states that in the case of compensation or compensation to farmers, state aid would not be present in the following two cases: If compensation or compensation were not financed from public funds, therefore, in the case of financing from the price of water, the State should not prescribe the purpose of the use of the funds, or if the State were liable to compensation for the damage suffered by farmers due to the introduction of water protection areas. The Ministry of Environment and Space has given an opinion on the first condition that the proposed Article 3 actually sets out the purpose of the use of resources, namely State aid, and on the second condition that the assessment of whether the State is liable for damages is virtually impossible to say that the regulation of the water protection regime is indeed a State compensation measure, since there is no unlawful conduct that would render the State liable for compensation. Perhaps later in the discussion, the Government will be able to explain these substantive reasons more precisely. In addition to the two conditions set out by the Ministry of Finance, the Ministry of Finance adds that it has concerns about compensation liability due to the following two facts. In the Guidelines on State aid in the agricultural and forestry sectors, the European Commission has taken the view that compensation for damage caused by restrictions related to the Water Directive constitutes State aid. The second point is that, in line with the practice of the European Commission and the Court of Justice of the European Union, the burden of amending legislation is one of the usual costs to be borne by companies. Notwithstanding this content, the position of the Ministry of Environment and Space relating to other substantive reasons is also interesting in the inter-departmental coordination for the reasons for the amendment of Article 3. Among them, let me mention just a few. A problem is the amount of compensation which would now no longer be determined, so if the article remained in force. In the absence of professional rules for the assessment of injury, it is thus impossible to predict or assess the amount of the compensation proposed and the type of crop that would result in the loss of income. Therefore, it would make sense — and this position in the Ministry of Environment and Space, of course, we support — to draw up certain professional recommendations at the professional level on the basis of which the taxpayer could calculate the amount of compensation and the beneficiary of the compensation could prove any greater damage only later in court. In addition, long-term solutions should be sought primarily in addressing the problem of farming in the water protection area. One of the ideas that also arose in the debate on the Committee was organic farming, and the payment of compensation or compensation should in fact be an extreme measure. At the same time, alternative agricultural land should be made available as a priority. This also raises the question whether the Agricultural Land Fund or other owners of agricultural land have a sufficiently large agricultural land fund that could be replaced. Furthermore, the existing Article 3 proposal also covered two new measures for which compensation was to be paid, namely a reduction in the value of agricultural land and a reduction in the value of agricultural holdings. Even for these two new measures, it is not possible to estimate roughly what their height would be and what this damage would involve. Furthermore, in the new proposal for amendments to the Act, it should be added that the beneficiary is obliged to amend all information relevant to the determination of compensation within a certain period of time, we imagine that this could be a period of 30 days from the date of the amendment, that the beneficiary is not entitled to any other compensation, subsidy, compensation or aid for the same or similar measure provided for in the second title, and that there is a certain period of time, the transitional provisions should set a time limit for the first submission of the request by the beneficiary or the transmission of the contract by the debtor. Article 17 is deleted because it provides for a transitional provision to Article 3. All the amendments mentioned above will be supported, as already stated in the presentation of the position. ParlaMint-SI_2015-07-14-SDZ7-Redna-10.ana.u55	The text is given by the Group of Social Democrats, Mrs Marija Bachič.
ParlaMint-SI_2015-07-14-SDZ7-Redna-10.ana.u56	Madam Vice-President, colleague and colleague. We have before us a bill amending the War Veterans Act. In 2012, the Public Finance Balance Act disproportionately affected the right to health care for war veterans. Zujf abolished the legal basis for the exercise of the right to medical and climate treatment, increased the age for the exercise of rights under the War Veterans Act from 50 to 55 years of age, and further increased the conditions for war veterans to exercise the right to health care. He permanently abolished the only material right of war veterans, which is the payment of the supplementary health insurance premium. Before the adoption of Zujf, the right was guaranteed to all war veterans who had reached the age condition of the War Veterans Act. The right to payment of the supplementary health insurance premium provided by the State budget remained unchanged under the terms of Zujf only for persons with the status of war invalid and for persons with the status of victim of war violence. The right to payment of the supplementary health insurance premium from 1 January 2013 was granted to war veterans only selectively when the age condition and conditions for obtaining the veteran supplement were met. In the Parliamentary Group of Social Democrats, we insist that the rights granted under the War Veterans Act constitute recognition of the Republic of Slovenia for the contribution given by the veterans of the war, the fighters of the NOB and members of the defence and security forces who participated in the liberation processes or the war for Slovenia in 1991 for the freedom and independence of Slovenia. When Zujf was adopted, they made great efforts to ensure that the provisions adopted in the law did not remain permanent. We believe that the receipts of war veterans and war victims must not and cannot be of a social nature and not constitute a payment, but rather a compensation for physical and mental injury, and mean a expression of gratitude. This is precisely why the then opposition MPs, with Matjaz Han, wanted to prevent the permanent withdrawal of those rights by the end of 2012 with the Zujf novel. The new law provided for a preliminary period in which these rights would be frozen in connection with economic growth. Unfortunately, all attempts to change Zuif failed. It was only in 2014 that the proposed solution passed the new Law on War Veterans, which re-examined rights, such as those before the adoption of Zujf, and bound them to a condition of economic growth, which would allow the payment of these rights to a greater extent and to realize the special constitutional protection of these beneficiaries. The novelty that we are referring to today and that has been put into the legislative process by the Group of Members and Members, with the first-signed Jozet Tank, provided for a solution to ensure that war veterans receive health services in the amount of the difference to the full value of the services in the framework of compulsory health insurance as soon as possible and that the condition of 2.5% of economic growth is deleted. As stated in 2014 the adopted amendment provided for this right to be returned on 1 January of the year following the year for which the economic growth found will exceed 2,5 % of the gross domestic product. This is a dictatorship that is unified with other such measures adopted under the Public Finance Balance Act. As the Government points out in its opinion, the exercise of rights from public funds is, indeed, about different content and under different conditions, but measures to balance public finances apply equally to all beneficiaries. By removing the procrastination of economic growth, however, war veterans would be in a significantly more favourable position than other citizens, beneficiaries of a variety of types of rights from public funds, which would only be felt by the effects of favourable economic growth in 2016. In addition, if the SDS proposal were to be adopted, additional budget resources of 58 million would be needed for this purpose, but are not provided for in the 2015 budget. In view of the above, the majority have already assessed on the parent’s labour body that the amendment of the law cannot be supported. Thank you.

The representative of the founder would be proposed by the Ministry responsible for agriculture, representative of users of the Agricultural Forestry Chamber of Slovenia and representative of workers. The president of the world would be elected by members of the world from among themselves. Secondly, it will be possible to classify equivalent candidates by introducing a general principle of order between equivalent candidates. Thirdly, the government's mandatory consent is also introduced for the sub-laws governing the lease. ParlaMint-SI_2015-07-14-SDZ7-Redna-10.ana.u72	Dear Vice President, colleagues and colleagues! The Group of 5 thousand electorates and voters submitted to the National Assembly the amendment of the Law on the Fund for Agricultural Lands and Forests of the Republic of Slovenia. Although it is a so-called people's initiative supported by voters' signatures, it is worth noting that it is a bill prepared by the Agricultural Forestry Chamber of Slovenia and that voters or signatories are mostly its members. It is an attempt by the Chamber to remedy the solutions adopted by the amendment to this Act in 2010. The Agricultural Forestry Chamber of Slovenia proposes to change representation in the management bodies of the Fund, which owns state-owned assets. It is worth noting that the 2010 National Assembly eliminated the conflict of interest between the private interests of members of the Council when it stipulated in Article 5(1) that the President and members of the Council of the Fund may not be persons for whom there is a potential conflict between their private interests and the public interests pursued by the Fund. The possibility of a conflict referred to in the preceding sentence shall be considered if the member or chairman of the Board is a member of the fund of a tenant or a tenant of land in the management of the fund or a contractual contractor of the works in the forests with which the fund is managed. The proposed solution therefore seeks to legitimize the situation before 2010. According to our assessment of the novel, he is trying to legislate solutions that would allow the Agricultural Forestry Chamber of Slovenia or its members to influence the management of state property, which is contrary to the interests of the fund's founder, i.e. the state. Such a composition of the world could therefore have an impact on the implementation of land policy if users or leasers of agricultural land, forests and farms from the fund currently exempt participate in the decision-making process. To this end, further proposed amendments also show that they introduce a mandatory order between equivalent priority beneficiaries, in order to follow the objectives of the development of the agricultural activity. In addition, the proposed amendment seeks to prevent the granting of state-owned free agricultural land by auctioning. The proposed changes are, according to our assessment, to the detriment of the Fund or its business policy and therefore also state property. In the Alliance, we agree to some extent that the Fund should also pursue the objectives of agricultural policy in the field of agricultural land transfer, which does not mean that it can completely ignore economic objectives. The data show that the average price of the purchase of state agricultural land is lower than the average price of the lease of private agricultural land and the return of the fund does not exceed 2 % of the value of the property of agricultural land, which means that the leases are still too low despite the increase. On the basis of bids, a three- to four-fold lease was reached. It is worth noting that market-oriented management of state property must also be enforced in the field of agriculture. This is a so-called natural income which the state cannot and must not leave to the private, even if they are engaged in an agricultural activity. For a long time, the Fund was a kind of parastatal fund, a political toy of one of the political parties that it used at the time of its participation in the government to satisfy the economic benefits of its members and sympathizers. Although it is not possible to report the latter to the Agricultural Forestry Chamber of Slovenia, we cannot agree to re-establish logic as socially acceptable, that state property is not to be treated marketably, and that it is permissible for the State to bring them in on the basis of the so-called pressurization of policies that are hidden behind public initiatives to individual stakeholders. In addition, it is necessary to be aware of the financial obligations of the Fund. Some 12 million Euros have been paid from denationalisation procedures due to the inability to use agricultural land and forests returned in denationalisation procedures and 22 million Euros have still been opened. In the future, this will represent a very large financial burden for the Fund and the question is whether it will be able to pay it itself and will have to be taken directly by the state. In addition to the need for the Fund to pay the denationalisation financial liabilities due to the inability to use land and forests, it must also allocate 10% of all revenues each year to pay for all denationalisation obligations. The last fund for these purposes shall be transferred to the previous Court or today’s SDH. Not to mention the rent chosen by the mayors of municipalities where forests owned by the Republic of Slovenia and the owners of the fund are located. This annual burden of over €3 million, which is even more than the fund has to allocate to the Soda or SDH. On the basis of the above arguments, we do not support the bill in the Alliance.

ParlaMint-SI_2015-07-14-SDZ7-Redna-10.ana.u80	Thank you, Madam Vice President. Dear Members and Members! The Government of the Republic of Slovenia forwarded to the National Assembly a proposal for amendments to the Law on Development Support to the Pomurska Region. The government has assessed the implementation of the law adopted by 22. Ten. 2009 and started to apply 1. 1. 2010, two more years to be extended, that is to 31. 12. 2017. Extension is the key and, in fact, the only substantive amendment to this law. It is accompanied by the harmonisation of the text due to changes in the organisation of the state administration. At the time of the adoption of the law, the competent national authority was the RS Government Service for Local Self-Government and Regional Policy, which is now the Ministry of Economic Development and Technology. We estimate that the law needs to be extended, which is based on a legal commitment resulting from the sixth paragraph of Article 4 of the Law. The law explicitly states that the basic measure of development support must be a programme to promote the competitiveness of the Pomeranian region, achieved by 2015 at a total value of EUR 33 million. The law provided the possibility that, in accordance with the budgetary options and needs for the implementation of the Government programme, other instruments could be identified by the programme or its amendment, which is also higher, but not lower, total amount, or long-term annual implementation dynamics as foreseen by this law. An estimated EUR 5 million and a half a year. At the same time, the law provides that the programme must be implemented at a total value of €33 million. By the end of 2014, €19 million had been paid and 4.4 million had been provided for this purpose in 2015. The calls have already been made. Therefore, for 2016 and 2017, around EUR 9.6 million remains to be allocated and paid. The reason for the delay in payments in the first years of implementation of the programme is mainly in the initial activities that took a certain start-up time to begin with a higher intensity. In recent years, however, the government has faced limited budgetary capacity, since the government could provide a significantly smaller share of the funding than the initially statutory €5.5 million per year in the years of the budget or the national budget. The implementation of the programme and the law shall also be monitored by the National Assembly in the framework of the annual reports. The Committee has already considered the 2013 annual report and there is an annual report for 2014 in the Government, so that it will soon be in the National Assembly. We note that most of the funding under the programme has been earmarked for instrument one, which is to encourage initial investment by businesses and job creation. We are aware that less activity has taken place in instruments two and three, that is to encourage the development of human resources and social entrepreneurship, and that in the framework of both instruments, which also represent more important untapped opportunities in this region, there will be the possibility of projects already established but not implemented in 2016 and 2017. The Government or the Ministry also envisages amending the Social Entrepreneurship Act, which has some of the downsides that have been shown at that time, in particular not to confuse specific pre-determined activities, but to open up and allow for innovation in this area. We will also propose in particular simplifications in the registration of social enterprises. From the previous annual reports and the current or current statistics in the Pomeranian region, in some areas a gradual improvement of the general economic picture is seen, but not to the extent that the extension of the period of implementation of the law is not justified. There is still a problem with high unemployment, which currently reaches 19.1% on average with April data. We therefore estimate that the extension of the law and the implementation of the programme totalling EUR 33 million is also appropriate from the point of view of the currently tightening public financial capacity. Following the adoption of the extension of the law on the development of the Pomurski region in the National Assembly, we will also propose a promotion programme for the extension and thus give the possibility for the Ministry of Economic Development and Technology to publish early in the spring of 2016 the first public tenders in the remainder of the value. Thank you.

On the basis of tangible economic impulses, mainly at the expense of the post-harvest exporters, who recorded a record EUR 665 million of exports in 2014, it appears that the regional economy is also collected from the development bottom at the expense of the pomurs law. In the region, there are almost 10 000 unemployed and recorded a 19.1% registered unemployment rate that exceeds the Slovenian average by good 53% and the per capita GDP by 34%. All of this shows that the consequences of the economic crisis due to economic weakness in the region are still strong. The shocks of the collapse of large companies are slowing down by strengthening medium-, small- and micro-enterprises that are already employing 70% of all employees and becoming increasingly competitive in the global market. It is essential for the region and the country to make use of the rights already guaranteed by law to use development incentives to the end, as only in this way can the law achieve the objective and justify the purpose. This will only be possible if the programming period is extended by two years or until the end of 2017. The main reason that the region could not benefit from the already guaranteed rights to draw on the development support programme for promoting the competitiveness of the Pomeranian region of EUR 33 million was the budgetary capacity of the country. By the end of 2015, the rebalancing of drawing rights will only benefit from €23.4 million and the remainder of €9.6 million by the end of 2017, which will not jeopardise the achievement of the objectives of the law, as we are opening up the possibility of granting support to quality, productive and sustainable projects by extending the period. We are aware of the importance of fulfilling the legal obligations towards the Pomeranian region and of consistent regional development in the Group of the Modern Centre Parties, and therefore support the amendment of the law, which is assessed by the region and the Government as good, useful and needed by the region. Thank you.

ParlaMint-SI_2015-07-14-SDZ7-Redna-10.ana.u90	Thank you for your word, Madam President. Ladies and gentlemen, honourable Members, the distinguished Secretary of State with the team! Before us is the first review of the Law amending the Law on Development Support to the Pomurski region in the period 2010-2015. The proposed amendment proposes to extend the period of implementation of the law by two years, until the end of 2017. The proposed law assesses that it will be easier to achieve the objectives of the law during the extension period, as well as to provide the necessary means to implement the Competitiveness Promotion Programme in 2016 and 2017. The Pomur Act was adopted in 2009 with the aim of introducing additional measures to promote the development of the Pomur region, the creation of new jobs and the preservation of existing jobs, as well as the establishment of development infrastructure and the elimination of the consequences of the economic and financial crisis in the Pomur region. The main objective of the law was and remains to reduce unemployment or create new jobs. But as the proposed amendments to the law themselves identify, the instruments that are the subject of this law have still not solved the central problem, this is a major unemployment in the Pomurska region. This is also confirmed by figures showing that the number of unemployed remains at 19 to 20%, which is the same as in the period of adoption of the law at the end of 2009. In the Slovenian Democratic Party, we supported the law at the time of its adoption in 2009, but we have already pointed out a number of concerns which, if we look at the results of the law today, have proved justified. Let me make the most of it. We opposed the establishment of a government project office, as this part could be taken over by a regional development development agency, which, by setting up a project office, was somewhat untrustworthy. We also assessed at that time that there would be a duplication of tasks on the part of the development agency and the project office, which is certainly not rational. Further, when the management of the government office was taken over by the then State Secretary from the cabinet of Prime Minister Andrej Horvat, it was clear why the establishment of the project office was necessary. Later, due to conflict of interest, suspicion of corruption and irregularities in the implementation of the pomur law, he, of course, resigned as Secretary of State. We also mentioned in the Slovenian Democratic Party that there is no need for the establishment of a regional economic centre to be subject to this law. Looking at the results today, we see that 21 million euros have been paid under this measure, but only for three projects of the development centres of the Slovenian economy, which have generated only 162 jobs and five new businesses. We do know, however, that one of these companies, the Development Centre for new materials, drives and technologies, has been bankrupt since March this year. We also opposed the establishment of an inter-enterprise education centre, as the tasks could be coordinated and carried out by the development agency in cooperation with interested associations and educational institutions. This would play a greater role in the existing system of educational institutions and development agencies and would rationalise the necessary activities in this area. Looking at the results of this measure today, we see that we have invested EUR 2 million in the adaptation of the facility and the purchase of technological equipment. So we only have a renovated and equipped facility. We do not see any results in the form of new jobs or the results of the development of the Pomur region. With regard to the water supply project in the Slovenian Democratic Party's Parliamentary Group, we have pointed out that this project was already in the national programme prior to the adoption of this law. Nevertheless, investing in drinking water infrastructure has become one of the measures where we know that there is a suspicion of fraud in construction linked to the unjustified accounting of labour and material and the fear of returning European funds. It is also possible to understand from the accompanying material of the proposed amendment of the law that the money to go towards the development of the Pomeranian region and to reduce the number of unemployed was spent on project tasks and evaluation studies allocated to the external contractor, which we have already opposed in the Slovenian Democratic Party's Parliamentary Group at the time of adoption and we also oppose today. It is correct to remind you, however, that in 2009, the SLS Group tabled an amendment that sought to ensure that funds for the development of the Pomeranian region were not allocated and used for the production of studies, expertise, professional bases, documentation and advisory contracts, which we also supported in the Slovenian Democratic Party's Group, but unfortunately the left coalition of this amendment did not support. Today we believe it was for a reason. A number of critical points of implementation of the law of purgatory have been highlighted, which in fact point to the non-implementation of the fundamental mission of this law. Regardless of the proposed change in the deadline for the implementation of the law on the developmental Pomeranian region in the first reading, the Slovenian Democratic Party's Group will be supported. However, I must make it clear that we strongly oppose the use of resources for various studies and advisory contracts. Thank you.

ParlaMint-SI_2015-07-14-SDZ7-Redna-10.ana.u93	Thank you very much. Mrs Marija Bacič will present the position of the Group of Social Democrats. Here you go.
ParlaMint-SI_2015-07-14-SDZ7-Redna-10.ana.u94	Thank you for your word, Mr. Vice President. Dear Secretary of State with a team, respectable colleagues and colleagues! The reason for the adoption of the intervention law in 2009 was the need of the urban development region to require State aid in view of the development indicators and the constitutional principle of the social state and solidarity with people in the financial and economic crisis. The economic power of Pomurje deteriorated significantly in 2008 as the impact of the financial and economic crisis further increased the development backlog. The Pomur economy was seized by financial and economic spasms, which were also present elsewhere in Slovenia, but because of economic weakness in Pomur, their impact was significantly stronger. It was found that only regional policy measures are not sufficient, but specific measures are needed, and in particular a coordinated action of sectoral policies. On the basis of an assessment of the situation in Pomurje, which has shown great difficulties, a development law has been drawn up which has provided public funds for measures to open new perspectives in Pomurje and provided a new opportunity for the gloomy economy to finally benefit from the comparative advantages of geothermal energy, sustainable agriculture and agri-food and, of course, tourism. The purpose of the law was to support the preparation and implementation of development projects in the region by enhancing the absorption capacity of Pomur in public tendering candidates and programmes. By investing, the public co-financing of companies' investments sought to encourage the development of businesses and entrepreneurship in Pomure. The desire was to create business dynamise the region and, above all, to ensure faster opening of new jobs. By investing in staff, the labour force in Pomour wanted to properly reclassify and train for new challenges and increase the possibility of new employment for educated staff and, by promoting social entrepreneurship, create conditions for the accelerated development of employment. The programme focused on improving the image of the region and promoting it with the aim of attracting foreign and domestic investors. The main solutions to the law thus provided adequate development support for the restructuring of the backward development regions of Pomur region, where the situation has deteriorated significantly in severe economic and financial conditions. With the proposed measures prepared under the programme approach and the provision of financial resources, the then government wanted to encourage investment in domestic and foreign capital and thus create the conditions for restructuring the area. The law provided for a specific programme to promote the competitiveness of the Pomeranian region for the period 2010-2015, which allocated EUR 33 million, and also envisaged incentives for employment and proposed tax incentives for investment and priority treatment of programmes and projects from Pomurje in the run for state and European funds and the establishment of a regional economic centre. The indicative value of the measures under the priority treatment of projects and programmes was EUR 162 million, of which EUR 90 million was the largest, was devoted to investing in infrastructure for the supply of healthy drinking water to the population. Investment, promotion and enhancement of the competitiveness of agriculture and forestry and the food and processing industry were estimated at EUR 40 million. However, EUR 30 million was foreseen to encourage the Regional Economic Centre in Pomour. Measures totalling EUR 261 million have been envisaged over 6 years. The main objective of the specific programme was to alleviate crisis situations and create thousands of new jobs, combined with different instruments. For Pomurje, the largest number of directly created new jobs in sectors with higher added value was and is still crucial. Some measures had an immediate effect; others were longer term in nature. The law certainly wasn't perfect, which doesn't mean he was unsuccessful. The analysis shows that the basic productivity indicators in Pomura are improving, but there is still a significant gap in relation to Slovenia's average. GDP per capita in the Pomurska region was 30.9% less than the Slovenian average in 2012. In some areas, the gradual improvement of the general economic picture is significant in the Pomur region, but not in the area of unemployment and not to the extent that it is not necessary to extend the period of implementation of the law. The registered unemployment rate according to the latest available data is 19,1 %, which is 7 % more than at national level. The consequences of the economic crisis remain strong due to economic weakness in the Pomurska region. The interim analysis of the implementation of the 2010-2013 programme responded to questions on the adequacy, effectiveness and effectiveness of the programme. It follows from the findings that the allocations were too low in order to make major structural changes in the region. While the instruments dealt adequately with some problems in the region, they did not solve the underlying problems of high unemployment. The Pomurje 2015 programme has appropriately upgraded and complemented other policies or programmes that are or are being implemented in Slovenia. The link between the appropriations and better business could not be statistically demonstrated, but business operations, particularly smaller ones, have improved. However, companies have increased their productivity, optimized the work process, increased production volume, reduced operating costs, increased flexibility and facilitated field work. The proposed amendment therefore extends the period of implementation of the law for two years. The programme to promote the competitiveness of the Pomeranian region in the period 2010-2015, for which EUR 33 million was foreseen, was carried out at 57.6%. Unrealised funds for the implementation of the programme amount to around €14 million, almost €4.4 million for 2015, and the remaining €9 million 600 are to be provided through the 2016 and 2017 national budgets. Since the objectives of the law have not yet been achieved and all the funds envisaged have not yet been used, the extension of the law and the subsequent adaptation of the development programme, which will allow for more efficient use of resources in the coming years, are justified. We believe that the proposed projects need to be implemented to the end, so we will support the proposed amendment of the law. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-SI_2015-07-14-SDZ7-Redna-10.ana.u104	Thank you very much, Mr Vice President. Dear colleagues and colleagues! Before preparing for today's debate, I reviewed certain policies of less developed areas as an element of a policy of more coherent regional development in Slovenia. The fact is that we can adopt a number of such individual sectoral laws that will favour an area, but until, in fact, the whole country has sufficient development potential, such laws will also not help to accelerate the development of an individual region, which is not least reflected in all these statistics, which do not differ significantly from other regions that have similar problems in terms of development opportunities. Last but not least, we have a lot of time in this country under the Harmonised Regional Development Act, which actually covers all these segments, which we then define in some special law. In fact, beyond this basic problem that this country has, that there is not enough resources in terms of financial resources for individual measures, we cannot. The adoption of a separate partial law for a particular area is only in some way a political game and sand in the eyes of an area, and in fact, given that there is no financial resources, such a law does not in practice contribute to any faster development of an individual area. This is also shown specifically in the case of the Court, where, despite the fact that the matter can be well thought out and some programmes contain large numbers, but later there are programmes that actually provide a fifth of the funds than this, and of course we all wonder later why certain things do not go faster. The same applies to the law of Pomur, and as a result this proposal is also intended to extend its implementation for the next two years. I think it will also be difficult in these two years to provide additional resources that would allow for faster development of this area, because, in view of all the pre-preparation so far for drawing on the new financial perspective, it does not show very well, since until 2017 nothing shows that any of the funds from this new financial perspective will be available. That is why this law will depend in particular on budgetary resources, and we will again come to a situation similar to that of previous laws that we have dealt with, especially the Water Act, where there has been a lot of talk about how we need to support some changes in legislation and this will improve some situation. However, this will only improve the situation if funds are also provided for individual measures. And here we are again before the dilemma of what the National Assembly will do when we have the budget documents on the table and when certain financial resources will have to be provided for the Pomur law. Otherwise, we can discuss and find out each month what is good, what is not basically good in the implementation of this law, but we are practically not doing much to make these results and developments in this area any better.

AT THE TIME OF THE DAY, THIS IS ANOTHER DEFINITIVELY PROPOSAL OF A LEGISLATION ON Acquisition AND TRANSFER OF PARTS OF HUMAN BODIES FOR HEALTH, IN THE context of a regular procedure. The draft law was tabled by the Government for consideration by the National Assembly. In order to further explain the proposal, I give the floor to the representative of the Government to the Minister of Health, Marija Milojka Kolar Celarc. Here you go.
ParlaMint-SI_2015-07-14-SDZ7-Redna-10.ana.u134	Good afternoon. Hello, Madam Vice President, Members and Members of Parliament! The draft law on the acquisition and transplantation of parts of the human body is adopted following a regular procedure. At the initiative of Members, a general debate was held in May and it was agreed that the proposal for a law was suitable for further consideration. On the basis of the opinion of the Legislative and Legal Service of the National Assembly, amendments were prepared which were adopted by the Health Committee and were mainly of a nominal nature. The proposal for a new law primarily seeks to ensure the quality and safety of human organs intended for transplantation and to lay down conditions for the smooth legal exchange of organs at the level of the European Union and European authorities for the exchange of organs with a view to reducing the risk of abuse or even trafficking in human organs. The objectives are also the requirements of the European directives, which we transpose into the Slovenian legal order. Thus, Directive 2010/53 EU of the European Parliament and of the Council on standards of quality and safety of human organs intended for transplantation and Commission Directive 2012, 25/EU on procedures for the transmission of information in the exchange of human transplant authorities between Member States. Another novelty passed through the law relates to the area of consent to a donation in the case of deceased persons, stipulating that any person who is able to decide on his or her own treatment may accept or oppose a donation in writing during the course of his or her life. The definition of giving is a novelty in the matter of giving. We are also correcting the disputed so-called supposed consent by means of the bill, so that the removal of parts of the human body after death is now possible only if the donor has consented to a gift for the time of life, or if he has not been identified as a gift during life, the person who died is notified of the gift. If this person agrees to the withdrawal, it shall be carried out, but if he disagrees, the withdrawal shall not be carried out. However, if those near death cannot be obtained, the seizure shall also not be carried out. The preparation of the law was carried out in cooperation with Slovenia as the competent authority in the field of transplantation in the Republic of Slovenia. Since 2000, Slovenia has been a member of the international network for the exchange of organs and tissues of Eurotransplant, which consists of seven other countries in addition to Slovenia. Participation in the large transplant network is also extremely important due to the relative smallness of Slovenia, as it provides solidarity assistance to the network in emergencies and helps to improve the outcome of transplants. We would like to point out that the transplant treatment system in Slovenia is fully transparent, professional and clearly defined by national legislative frameworks, which clearly show the ethical imperative and protect against the abuse of both donors and recipients of tissue organs. I would like to thank you here for such support for all of you Members in the adoption of the law, which we are very happy to make a better and safer health system in the Republic of Slovenia together. And given that no amendment to the amended bill has been tabled, we propose that a third consideration of the bill be made at today's meeting. Thank you.
ParlaMint-SI_2015-07-14-SDZ7-Redna-10.ana.u135	The draft law was treated by the Health Committee as a working mother. I give the floor to Vice-President Branko Zorman to submit the report to the Committee.
ParlaMint-SI_2015-07-14-SDZ7-Redna-10.ana.u136	Honorable Prime Minister, distinguished Minister, Ministry team, colleagues, colleagues! The Health Committee shall meet at its sixth regular session of 24. 6. 2015 as a parent working body, considered the proposal for a law on the acquisition and transplantation of parts of the human body due to treatment submitted by the Government to the National Assembly for consideration and reception. The Committee took note of the following material: a proposal for a law published on the website of the National Assembly in the Rapporteur of 2. 4. 2015, by the opinion of the Legislative and Legal Service of 9. 6. 2015, opinion of the Ministry of Health on the opinion of the Legislative and Legal Service of 17. 6. 2015 and the opinion of the Commission of the National Council for Social Protection, Labour, Health and Disabled Persons of 22. 6. This Decision shall enter into force on the twentieth day following that of its publication in the Official Journal of the European Union. The representative of the proposer, State Secretary at the Ministry of Health, Nina Pirnat, put forward in a supplementary explanation the main solutions to the bill and cited the main reasons for the new system law. The representative of the Legal and Legislative Service, Dr. Samo Dijak, stressed that the comments of the ZPS were given a written opinion by the Ministry of Health. He noted that the drafter cited the adopted Directive 2010/45/EU of the European Parliament and of the Council on standards of quality and safety of human organs intended for transplantation, which requires Member States to harmonise national legislation with European legal rules up to 27. 8. 2012 and Implementing Directive 2012/25 of the European Commission on procedures for the transmission of information in the exchange of human transplant authorities between Member States, to be implemented by Member States in national law by 10. 4. 2014 and that both of these deadlines have already expired. Specific comments to each article were taken into account in the amendments of the coalition groups. Representative of the Commission of the National Council for Social Protection, Labour, Health and Disabled Mags. Stevo Ščavčičnik stressed that the Commission supports the proposal of the law. The debate raised the question whether the drafter also aligned himself with the Commission on Medical Ethics, in particular in the area of parts of the body of the deceased embryo. Slovenia is a signatory to the Ovied Convention, the Convention on Human Rights in the field of biomedicine and its additional protocol on the transplantation of human organs, which bypassed the rights of the embryo as the Protocol does not apply to the organs and tissues of embryos and foetuses. For ethical reasons, parts of the body of a decommissioned embryo should not be allowed to be taken away in the event that pregnancy is allowed to stop. Since euthanasia is prohibited in Slovenia, further regulation of the prohibition of transplantation of organs from euthanised persons is unnecessary. At the beginning of its cooperation with Eurotransplant, Slovenia agreed not to accept the organs of euthanised donors, so it does not even offer these organs of Slovenia. The amendment of the coalition's parliamentary group, the United Left and the NSI were tabled within the time limit set for the bill. The Committee adopted amendments to the coalition parliamentary groups and the Committee did not adopt amendments to the United Left and NSI groups. In accordance with Rule 128 of the Rules of Procedure of the National Assembly, the Committee voted on all the articles of the bill and adopted them. In the light of the amendments adopted, the text of the amended legislation, which includes the amendments adopted, has been prepared pursuant to Article 133(1) of the Rules of Procedure of the General Assembly. The amended bill is an integral part of this report.

The management of these diseases requires an integrated approach and is organised both by countries and by all other stakeholders. We are also driven by changes in the environment that are visible at every step. Unhealthy food supply dominates health and food advertising with a high sugar content, salt, fat is very aggressive, and there is also greater physical inactivity.
