In Slovenia, we estimate that the direct cost of healthcare for only diabetes, which is about 7% of the population, is at least €114.3 million per year, which is almost 5% of all the funds available to our health department. If we do not act effectively, the number of patients will increase by about 3% each year. Let me also add that last year, compared to 2010, the consumption of cardiovascular medicines increased by 5.2%. The annual expenditure on medical bills was therefore over €80 million. ParlaMint-SI_2015-07-14-SDZ7-Redna-10.ana.u157	The following is a presentation of the views of the groups of Members. Mr Igor Zorčič has the floor of the modern centre's Parliamentary Group, for which again Mr Igor Zorčič has the floor.
ParlaMint-SI_2015-07-14-SDZ7-Redna-10.ana.u158	Thanks again. The purpose of the proposal for a law amending and supplementing the Law on the Transport of Dangerous Goods is to implement international rules, in particular the European Agreement on the International Carriage of Dangerous Goods by Road, which regulates the carriage of dangerous goods. The draft law regulates the obligations of participants in the transport of dangerous goods, provides for additional anti-movement insurance between the driver's duties, which is important for reducing the consequences of accidents, and the appointment of a safety adviser for the transport of dangerous goods. The Law also introduces new innovations concerning the appointment of safety advisers, since, in accordance with the European Agreement and in accordance with the Law on the organisation and financing of education and education, professional training of national professional qualifications advisers is regulated by a safety adviser/consulter for the transport of dangerous goods. The draft law lays down the conditions to be met by the authorised inspection organisations for vehicles carrying dangerous goods, vehicles shall comply with the requirements set out in the Annexes to the European Agreement on the International Carriage of Dangerous Goods by Road, the so-called ADR, with the authorisation of the professional training organisation of drivers for the transport of dangerous goods and the issue of certificates of competence to be determined on the basis of a public invitation to tender. The law further provides that the authority to issue a form for the review of vehicles for the transport of dangerous goods from the Ministry of Internal Affairs shall be transferred to the Ministry responsible for transport. The competence and designation of the authority responsible for nuclear safety for the transport of radioactive substances shall be regulated. It is also supplemented by the competence of the inspectorate responsible for the supervision of traffic regulations, as well as by criminal sanctions if the authorised organisation conducts checks on vehicles or provides professional training to drivers for the transport of dangerous goods in contravention of the law. In the Group of the Parties of the Modern Centre, we are convinced that the proposed law provides greater security for participants and general safety in the transport of dangerous goods, so the proposed law will be supported by the Group of Members. Thank you.
ParlaMint-SI_2015-07-14-SDZ7-Redna-10.ana.u159	Mr Ljubo Žnidar, the Slovenian Democratic Party's Parliamentary Group, has the floor.
ParlaMint-SI_2015-07-14-SDZ7-Redna-10.ana.u160	The proposal for a Law on the transport of dangerous goods is coordinated by the law in force with the amendments to the international European agreements. They shall regulate the transport of dangerous goods by means of regulations so that authorised vehicle inspection and driver training organisations are identified on a public invitation to tender. It further mentions the designation of authorities for specific areas, enhances the effectiveness of the implementation of the law and provides adequate control over the law itself. There have also been some concerns on the parental committee about a relatively long transitional period, which is 5 years from the entry into force of this law. During this period, the Minister will designate authorised organisations for the inspection of vehicles for the transport of dangerous goods and for the conduct of professional training of drivers. The applicant explains that it is necessary for the current authorised inspection and training organisations of drivers to obtain all the necessary accreditations and that, after the transitional period, these organisations will have to recommit themselves to tender. The Slovenian Democratic Party's parliamentary group will support the proposal for a law on the transport of dangerous goods.
ParlaMint-SI_2015-07-14-SDZ7-Redna-10.ana.u161	The text is given by the Group of the Democratic Party of the Pensioners of Slovenia, Mr Ivan Hršak.
ParlaMint-SI_2015-07-14-SDZ7-Redna-10.ana.u162	Thank you for your word, Madam President. Dear Minister, colleagues and colleagues! With amendments and additions to the Law on the Transport of Dangerous Goods, which lays down the minimum conditions to be met by all involved in the transport of dangerous goods, the law in force shall be in accordance with the amendments to international agreements. The amendment proposes to select dangerous goods vehicles for the public tender after the new authorised inspection organisations. One part of the amendments is also intended to increase the effectiveness of the implementation of the law or to ensure adequate supervision and to indicate the general designation of the authorities responsible for certain areas. At the time of consideration of the bill, we had concerns in our parliamentary group about a relatively long transitional period of 5 years from the entry into force of the law, in which the Minister will determine the authorised organisations for the review of the suitability of vehicles for the transport of dangerous goods or for the professional training of drivers for the transport of dangerous goods. The applicant has satisfactorily explained to us that a slightly longer period for switching to a different way of selecting authorised organisations is set because the current 4 authorised vehicle inspection organisations or driver training are authorised with an indefinite period of validity. An additional reason for the longer transitional period is that an authorised organisation must carry out checks on vehicles carrying dangerous goods of accreditation obtained in a specific and slightly longer procedure. If any new organisation wished to obtain new accreditation, it would allow them to participate in the public tender for a longer transitional period. On a concrete question: When will you stop co-financing renewable sources of electricity based on photovoltaics, based on panels imported from China, let me answer that the Eco Fund does not provide grants to co-finance solar electricity production, and for these purposes only re-funds are available to investors in the form of loans. Such an incentive aims to increase the share of renewable energy sources and consequently reduce greenhouse gas emissions, but, as I mentioned, only through recoveries. When are we going to stop the subsidies for burning wood biomass from industrial wood? The Ministry of the Environment and Space keeps reminding of the effects that increased use of wood biomass for air quality can have. Although the energy use of wood has a positive effect on reducing CO2 emissions and thus mitigating climate change. When burning wood as well as other biomass, CO2 emissions are released, but later, when new biomass is growing, this carbon binds to the plant again, reducing the share of CO2 in the air. The positive effect of wood on the reduction of CO2 emissions is mainly observed in the replacement of fossil fuels, which, by the way, are irreversibly burdening the climate with CO2 emissions. With a view to achieving synergies between climate change mitigation and improving air quality, in line with the requirements of Directive 2008/50/EC, in the air quality plans for the areas of Murska Sobota, Maribor, Celje, Kranj, Ljubljana, Novo mesto and for the three Sava municipalities, we have fully addressed the challenge you are facing. Through incentives from the country for the thermal rehabilitation of buildings, on the one hand, we are seeking to reduce the need for thermal energy throughout Slovenia, and incentives for the purchase of small combustion plants are only possible in sub-areas where remote heating and gas heating are not defined as a priority. The replacement of small combustion plants is therefore encouraged in these seven areas only in the remaining uninhabited areas. In doing so, I stress that modern combustion plants also have up to 40% greater efficiency than obsolete and simple, and improvements in air quality are even more favourable. Furthermore: How will you subsidize wood products as a renewable natural resource? We agree with the direction you are referring to, namely, that wood is primarily used for products of high added value and only then as fuel in small combustion plants. From the point of view of the incentives of the Eco Fund, I would like to point out that the Eco Fund has been promoting only wooden windows for several years, and that even with incentives for comprehensive rehabilitation and new construction, such as passive houses, the incentive is greater if natural materials are used. We have also done a lot at the Ministry in recent years in the field of the use of wood in the construction of buildings, and all the newer technical regulations have been completely or, as far as possible, removed restrictions on the use of wood in the construction of buildings from the point of view of ensuring the safety of users. In the area of building buildings, which are built from public funds, a step has already been taken more since the Green Public Procurement Regulation explicitly favoured the use of wood. In the future, we are planning a thorough overhaul of that Annex to the Green Public Procurement Regulation which regulates the construction of buildings. It is envisaged that the content represented by wood construction, this is sustainable construction, is also extended to other aspects, thus further encouraging the rational use of natural resources, of course also wood. How we will stop the financing of large infrastructure facilities through the Eco-Fund, I propose to say this further.

ParlaMint-SI_2015-07-14-SDZ7-Redna-10.ana.u240	The Minister for Labour, Family, Social Affairs and Equal Opportunities Dr. Anja Kopač Dark is responsible.
ParlaMint-SI_2015-07-14-SDZ7-Redna-10.ana.u241	Thank you, Mr President, for your word. Thank you, Mr. Franz Breznik, for the question. I will first answer how the prices of institutional protection programmes are determined. These are established on the basis of a methodology for the pricing of social security services, taking into account elements for the calculation of service prices such as labour costs, material and services costs, depreciation and investment support costs and financing costs. Coordination takes place once a year, on 1 March, on the basis of the Official Journal of the published Decision, Growth in the Prices of Social Security Services and the Decision establishing the Standard of Average Wages and the Costs and Expenditures of Institutional Protection Services for Persons over 65 years of age and for Adults with Special Needs in Special Social Security Institutions and Home Units for Older Persons, which can be taken into account in the Prices of Services. In doing so, labour costs are coordinated, in line with the changes in collective agreements and other acts for certain salaries and the costs of material and service costs in line with the annual consumer price index. In 2015, the cost of labour and material costs as part of the price calculation have not changed significantly. According to the rules of procedure, institutions have the possibility to apply the costs of depreciation of investment maintenance and financing in price, taking into account the limits set by the rules. Therefore, institutions could raise the price mainly from the last mentioned costs. After a review of how many institutions have decided to raise, these are among the rare because, for example, older people’s homes are very careful not to raise prices because this can cause them to lack capacity. When we look at what has been happening in this area in recent years, we find that social institutions, those for special needs, social security, and the homes for the elderly have not been raised. So this case you're referring to is probably one of the rare cases, but they have discretion, but the world of the institution needs to confirm such a price rise. However, since you have also linked this to the costs of the municipalities, I may be able to give just one general assessment. As the Government, we have reached an agreement, and the Ministry of Labour contributes as much as possible to this agreement, but we point out that we are in favour of reducing the administrative costs of municipalities, and we would certainly not want the costs of municipalities to be reduced in such a way as to remove the rights that municipalities provide to citizens and citizens or, if you wish, to citizens and citizens. One of these latest cases was the removal of the profit subsidy. In the final stage, indeed, the congregations will no longer have costs, and the state will not cover this – who is at a disadvantage? Citizens and citizens, citizens and citizens. While we are reducing costs, it is certainly necessary to look at all that administratively unnecessary, and by no means by reducing and harmonising the average paid to municipalities, we would not want to do so at the expense of citizens and citizens. And that's our common concern. I believe the mayors and mayors as well. Thank you.

ParlaMint-SI_2015-07-14-SDZ7-Redna-10.ana.u259	Dear Members and Members! Dear Member Zorman. At the time of its application, the payment system we have today had three objectives: a uniform, homogeneous pay system, a fair distribution of jobs and a stimulating pay system. We've maintained a single system. As far as fairness is concerned, we are already finding that there is a whole range of anomalies in distributions, but with regard to stimulation we have completely missed this goal, because I am also assessing that it is not too stimulating. Therefore, through proposals already based on government decisions that are the basis for discussions with trade unions this year to correct systemic legislation, and from proposals for concrete amendments to the Law on the wage scheme for public servants, which we have sent to the trade unions just days ago, and we will think that tomorrow we will be talking about it further, it turns out that we want to increase the variable part of the salary in two parts, in one part of the performance, and in the other part of the definition of the higher pay grade, while at the same time we want to reduce the number of automatic advances that are now ten pay grade for titles, which means that in the same job you can only get in practice given that you are a long time in this job, including up to fifteen and more wage classes of wage difference. We want to reduce these automatisms and spill the award into these two forms of performance and determine the temporary higher pay grade. However, the basis for this determination can be two, one is to do work in higher quality, it is said that you do your job extremely well, it is also considered to be kind, effective, interconnected and so on not only professionally good, but to do a greater amount of work, which means that you have done more work than your colleagues or colleagues have done. At the same time, we are also introducing, we are proposing the Institute of Prosperity, which means that those automatic advances, which we now propose to be only five more classes, can be performed automatically but can be held if the worker fails to achieve the expected results of the work, if this happens again next year, we can also cancel the contract to such a worker because he has not achieved the expected results of the work. There is an opportunity already in the current payroll legislation, but it is only very difficult to use, but there is a possibility. The great reservation to the introduction of a new pay system or incentive work, I think, is also the reason why it has not been successful, is that the leaders have not played their part. For the present pay system is based on the determination of the leader, and is completely subjective. Therefore, in recent years, or in recent years, in negotiations to reduce wages, we have simply sacrificed these stimulating parts in the first place, because they have also proved that we do not agree to this either by the leader or by the workers, so that someone can determine how much I have earned. And that is why we are in a situation today that we have an average public sector assessment, I think, 4,93 out of a total of 5, which means that we are all more than excellent workers. And if you ask yourself, according to the estimates of this work, then we have the best public administration in the world, if you just judge by the estimates, we know that in practice it is not. In order to effectively carry out this assessment of the variable work, however, the key is to set goals and to set criteria that everything depends not only on the objective assessment of the leader, but that it is necessary for each payee to determine what is what distinguishes a good doctor from an excellent one, which distinguishes a good teacher from an excellent teacher. Therefore, the main content of the talks with trade unions this year is to agree on what the criteria for awarding performance are. Without criteria, it will not work because we do not really trust the leader's personal, subjective assessments. However, it is important that these criteria are also subject to leadership at all levels. Because I also need to achieve certain goals as a leader and achieve certain criteria. Maybe I could just briefly comment on the union's position. They reject the changes because the current system is focused on the job. These changes that we are proposing, and we want them to move gradually in the direction of work, so you don't get paid for the job, but you get paid for the job. I believe that this process will continue for a while, and we also need an intensive dialogue with the trade union side. Thank you.

With the adoption of the Regulation amending and supplementing the Regulation on the bodies of the composition of the ministries, the Agency of the Republic of Slovenia for the Environment was given jurisdiction in last December to manage water lands and began with the resolution of the applications of private law and local communities for exchange, redemption, sale of land held in official records as water land. This has been the case in recent years — as you yourself have mentioned — for many years when these applications were neither approved nor processed — 650 applications were made. The real estate plan for 2015 includes 100 of them, of which 20 have already been entered into legal transactions, currently in the 9 applications process and in the preparation of 70 contracts. However, the resolution of applications is slower than planned, also due to the problem that will be presented in more detail below. The Water Act in Article 11 defines inland water as certain parts of the land with the prescribed characteristics. This prescribed property is that there is water on them, so by law natural or built water are public good. This status is also included in the Land Book, but water lands may be owned by individuals of private or public law. Those owned by the State are not in legal traffic. Thus, in the case of the resolution of applications for cases where, due to possible interference with the watercourse or also to the natural functioning of the water, the stream has moved away, leaving some area in some part, and is itself a stream or a water source, paved the way for new land. And so, on the one hand, we have the former, the past waterland and, of course, now this new, water running through. This water land is usually in ownership status of these areas, so that a new shaft on land is in the private sector, while it is abandoned, in many cases it is already covered and in public good water land. And in such cases itself, it often occurs, is the contractor itself, that is, the Agency of the Republic of Slovenia for the Environment, has encountered a problem, as notaries refuse to approve a contract without a decision or confirm an administrative unit, pursuant to Article 22 of the Agricultural Lands Act. On the basis of the land use certificates, they find that it is in accordance with Article 2 of the Agricultural Lands Act for purposeful use for agricultural land, which is also in nature now, since we have mentioned that it is a land where water is no longer present. However, this conclusion means that such land should be in accordance with the procedures prescribed by the Agricultural Lands Act, we are sometimes on the administrative unit said, and loaded on the notice board for 30 days and could be applied for purchase or exchange by anyone. And then, in accordance with the Agricultural Lands Act, it is determined who has the advantage of acquiring such land. The state doesn't have that advantage. We are therefore looking for an opportunity to resolve this conflict, as the Water Act states that it is a matter of the water land that the legislator wanted to settle with a specific law, Article 19 of the Water Act shows that the management of former water land is subordinated to the objective of establishing an optimum state of law as a result of the change in the location of water. The State is interested in owning water land and other persons own land that is not burdened by a restrictive water land regime. In this concept, the system of pre-emptive rights under the Agricultural Lands Act [[turn off the microphone]]

ParlaMint-SI_2015-07-14-SDZ7-Redna-10.ana.u285	This system of pre-emptive rights under the Agricultural Lands Act is a foreigner who equates the Water Act's objective. Therefore, a former water land intended for agricultural use may, by means of the Institute of Pre-Trial Rights, acquire a person outside the circle of persons defined in Article 19, paragraphs 4 and 5, on water. We are trying to take into account in the special regulation of water management, which is the category of actual use, and agricultural land, which is the category of actual and intended use, in particular the latter. But the legislator did not foresee the complications we encountered. It is necessary to determine whether both arrangements can be interpreted in a mutually consistent manner that eliminates the apparent conflict between the two laws or whether appropriate legislative changes need to be made. If it turns out that legal changes are needed, we will propose to implement them in the Agricultural Lands Act, as it is currently in the inter-sectoral coordination of the Proposal for Amendments to the Agricultural Lands Act, which would mean that during this year these 100 applications could be resolved and more actively devoted to the resolution of applications from 10 to 20 years of age, so 550 would remain in the following year. ParlaMint-SI_2015-07-14-SDZ7-Redna-10.ana.u325	Dear Member, mag. Marko Pogačnik, SDH began the process of refinancing and reprogramming the existing long-term liabilities due in 2015 and 2016 at a total of EUR 455 million and are secured by the guarantee of the Republic of Slovenia at the beginning of this year. The SDH assessed that the inflows from the sale of capital investments, including inflows of the first privatisation package, would not be sufficient to settle the outstanding long-term loans and legal obligations. On 23 January this year, the Ministry of Finance issued an agreement to initiate a total debt procedure of up to EUR 455 million, either by borrowing from business banks or by issuing debt securities, i.e. bonds without the guarantee of the Republic of Slovenia. Following the implementation of the procedure and the acquisition of tenders, SDH made the following findings for both the long-term liability of business banks and the organisation of the issuance of debt securities. Business banks are prepared to extend existing liabilities due to payment in 2015, which are collateral with a guarantee of the Republic of Slovenia of EUR 300 million, that is to say under the Republic of Slovenia Guarantee Act for liabilities of SDH of EUR 300 million for leased loans and issued bonds to finance the Court. In 2010, it is possible to extend existing commitments and guarantees for a period of two years. In addition, they obtained offers for the organisation of the issuance of debt securities and assessed that they could thus refinance and at the same time extend the debts due in 2016 and cannot be extended by the guarantee of the Republic of Slovenia. The volume of the issue was estimated at up to €150 million. The bond was successfully issued at a rate of EUR 100 million at a coupon fixed rate of 2.5 % and will be intended to repay existing debts due in 2016. This includes 135 million of these liabilities with the guarantee of the Republic of Slovenia leased at interest rates of 3- or 6-month Eurosystem plus 2.4 to 3.35%. The bonds were issued without State guarantee, which I've already said. In view of the fact that SDH was able to repay the issue of bonds at an appropriate amount, with a favourable interest rate and without State guarantees, I believe that SDH’s debt by issuing the bonds was prudent. The refinancing of the remaining financial liabilities due in 2016 will be carried out gradually, in line with the evolution of the settlement of commitments and the liquidity of the SDH plan. We are aware that the capital investment management strategy that has just been adopted partially interferes with SDH's free use of its capital investments, so we are planning to replace the portfolio, investments between the Republic of Slovenia and SDH at the end of 2016 and 2017, which will ensure the appropriate liquidity of SDH to cover obligations under the laws adopted.

ParlaMint-SI_2015-07-14-SDZ7-Redna-10.ana.u347	Thank you, Madam President. Dear Members and Members! Dear Mrs Dimic, thank you for this question. Let me say that the tasks and competences of the congregations are regulated, we have checked precisely, in about 100 laws in this country, and that they are all described, I think it was said earlier that about 100 were appointed by the congregation. I can say that the folder that prescribes congregation assignments is about three inches [7 cm] in length, so this task is much more complex. In fact, at the Ministry of Public Administration, the starting points for any change in these tasks are raised as a question in the strategy for the development of local self-government, which is in the final stage of preparation, and in these days we are writing the final version of the text of the draft strategy. However, I must reiterate that we did not go through a local self-government strategy as a prepared text into a public debate, but rather as open questions where all stakeholders, particularly local communities, are also asked what their views are. The municipalities shall exercise certain powers under the law and certain tasks conferred on them by the State. However, it is very important here that we apply the principle of subsidiarity, which means that we are always asking ourselves whether we can transfer the tasks and powers of the local community to the local community in such a way that they are done as close to the citizens as possible. So if we transfer tasks back to the country and centrally regulate certain powers, then we question the principle of subsidiarity because we want the tasks to be carried out as closely as possible to the citizens. I must also point out that the analyses have been carried out on how tasks are carried out in local communities, because we often face criticism that local communities are not doing their job, are not doing their job, and we find that actual tasks in the field of local communities are doing well, in a very large majority of them very well, and that local interests often also have an impact on which tasks they give more emphasis. But this is not least the purpose of the local community. The aim of the local community is to give some local emphasis and some specific priorities, but, of course, the tasks provided for under the law are exempted from this, so that they must be carried out in this way. The means for these tasks are fixed on average, as you yourself have determined. This average, when calculated, included a set of tasks, these tasks were weighted, and on the basis of these weightings also certain funds, and at that time I think that two or three even constitutional opinions on the subject were appropriate, and all constitutional opinions found that the existing method of financing was appropriate. Of course, local communities, like the state, may still be facing financial reality to a greater extent than the state budget, which means that we need to carry out these tasks within relatively limited financial options. However, if the situation were not so black, it might still be said that even today, it seems to me that they are able to pay the dues of that legal obligation, which is strictly prescribed, because we find that, precisely when you are helping at home, because you mentioned the area of the elderly where the responsibilities of the local community of 50% co-financing are prescribed, we are achieving this co-financing somewhere over 70% at the local community level, and, say, in paying for kindergartens and similar matters, I think that at the Ministry of Finance we have found that about 21 million payments are more than that strict legal obligation. This is good because the municipalities thus relieve their population, while at the same time confirming the fact that there is no problem that municipalities will be underfunded. However, what has already been identified in the questions already being sent today is a problem, very small municipalities, which, because of some specific payments they have to make, can actually become very vulnerable because of their smallness. Because, for example, one small congregation has two elderly people, and another small congregation has, say, 15 elderly people in household care, and this poses a major problem for it. But this is a problem for which I find it difficult to see this moment a systemic solution, except in the sense that the obligations paid by the congregations are also being combined. We will also try to find solutions in this direction in the local self-government strategy, in order to integrate the municipalities – not politically, but also in terms of the performance of the tasks – more stability in meeting the obligations that come from the laws and tasks that are passed on to them. Thank you.

And here is one option where we can provide 10,000 jobs with some smart policy that needs to be closed. I really believe that in 2017 this thing would go into the field of implementation and also ensure this long-term care. Without the full participation of all, i.e. intersectoral connections, this is not possible in fact, because it proved to be already at the time of Borut Pahor's mandate when it began to work very intensively in this area, but it has turned out that without the full support of the Government, the sector itself simply cannot do so. I believe that this government today has this support and that we will guarantee it. Thank you.

On the basis of data, reports and analyses, an annual progress report shall be prepared on the achievement of the NAM objectives with recommendations for future activities. In the last year, 2014 the number of beekeepers with application was 9, and the number of stumps affected was 247. There were also some reports this year, but as you've noticed, it was not a major nightmare, it was an individual. Before I start answering your four questions, I just want to say something. Unlike the general belief that these are harmful things that farmers do to bees and to nature, in the vast majority of cases this is not the case. The sanctions that a farmer may receive when using illicit starch, too starchy or at an irregular time, are so large, they are financially very large, they also mean the collection of direct payments and other payments of agricultural policy years ago, that we simply do not face such infringements anymore, in particular not much. The problem is the hobby of farmers and farmers who sprinkle on their gardens, on their three or four apple trees, and this is significantly harder to control. In this area, it will be necessary to move forward. As far as your questions are concerned. The Commission was set up in 2012. It consists of 29 experts. I must say, she's met several times. This report has created a problem, you have correctly identified it, and this problem has been that statistics have begun to collect data on users. And this was a joint task of the Statistical Office of the Republic of Slovenia and the Directorate for Safe Food in 2014. And then an extensive survey of spending took place, involving 10,000 users. The results of the processing are still not available, we are waiting for them and we will include them in this report, which is here before me. This is the 2013 and 2014 report, which is a report prepared as a draft, and we are waiting for this data to be included. Once the data are included, this expert group will first review the report, look at what to prepare at this time, and then, like all other documents of the Ministry of Agriculture, Forestry and Food, these data will also be made available to the public. And there's something else you need to say. The national programme itself was the basis for the adoption of the environmental part of the Rural Development Programme. You are aware that Slovenia, as a number of other countries, has been implementing this programme only since 2015. This is the first country we've been granted the programme. Some countries are still unable to implement it this year and will not be able to implement it until 2016. It will also be possible to assess which measures are sufficient and which measures are insufficient. Since I still have some time at my disposal, allow me to do the following. Of course, the programme and objectives need to be changed only when the results are seen. As regards data on atrazine and certain other substances, it should be said that the greatest burden is still some substances that have been banned for more than ten years. Unfortunately, these are substances that are slowly decomposing in the environment. On the other hand, the state of drinking water is very good, even if the potential effects of the use of plant protection products are taken into account. Even in those cases where they are found to be derogating, they are mainly microbiologically appropriate and nitrate content rather than plant protection products. Thank you.
